A stamp like `(v: 2026-08-14)` means "last confirmed on that date" — it is a staleness hint, not authority. When prose and `git`/MCP disagree, **git and MCP win** (#376).

## 🔒 SECURITY INVARIANTS *(check on EVERY DB or handler change — these must NOT regress)*

Non-negotiables from the 2026-06-18 security remediation. Re-check any time you add a table, policy, handler, or function — do not let a new feature quietly undo these:

1. **Every new public table = RLS deny-all + verify.** Create it with `enable row level security` + `create policy "Deny all access" … for all to public using (false)` in the SAME migration, then confirm with an anon-key probe (`curl --head -H "apikey:<anon>" -H "Authorization:Bearer <anon>" -H "Prefer:count=exact" "<base>/rest/v1/<table>?select=*"` → must be `Content-Range: */0`). The anon key is PUBLIC and `anon`/`authenticated` hold full table grants, so RLS is the ONLY thing protecting the DB. (gotcha #141)
2. **Every member-facing AUTH handler = caller-ownership check on `auth.callerMemberNumber`, NEVER a body id.** The edge fn runs as service-role (bypasses RLS), so a handler that reads/writes by a body `client_id`/`ciq_id`/`enrollment_id` without re-checking ownership is an IDOR. Reuse `utils/client-ownership.ts` (`denyIfNotOwnClient`/`denyIfNotOwnEnrollment`) + `actions/ciq/shared.ts denyIfNotOwnCiq`. Role-gates are deny-by-OMISSION + matched by EXACT action name. (gotcha #142) **The same principle extends to every deny-by-default portal role whose allowlist is broad:** a `tax_planner` caller (2026-07-22) skips the tab + ADMIN_ONLY gates, so every planner-callable tax handler MUST also carry an in-handler `denyIfNotPlannerClient`/`denyIfNotPlannerPlan` GROUP-scope guard (`utils/tax-planner-ownership.ts`) — the allowlist alone is not an ownership check (gotcha #257). **Deny-by-OMISSION cuts both ways — an action in NO gate list is UNGATED, not safe:** `vault_upload_url` minted signed writes into any `member-vault`/`member-tax-returns` folder off a body `member_number` while sitting in no list at all, until it was added to `MEMBER_SCOPED_ACTIONS` on 2026-07-30. Every signed-upload-URL minter must be provably covered by a **named** confinement — either grep `role-gates.ts` and name the gate (gotcha #309), or, for a PUBLIC token page, show that the destination bucket + path are derived **entirely server-side from the token row** and that the request body selects nothing (`vault_tax_upload_url`, and the 2026-07-30 `vault_request_upload_url` whose `vault_upload_tokens` row is its whole credential — gotcha #310). A minter with neither is a hole.
3. **Every new SECURITY DEFINER function = pin `search_path` + `REVOKE EXECUTE … FROM public`.** (gotcha #143)
4. **After ANY DB/table/policy/function change → run the Supabase security advisor (`get_advisors`, type `security`) and confirm it's GREEN** — this is the automated net that catches a regressed (anon-reachable) table before it ships. It is part of the VERIFICATION GATE below.

Full audit history + live-verified detail: the 2026-06-18 security-remediation entry in [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md) + gotchas #141–#146.

Four `|ach_verify` templates (plain + member-paying twin each, recipients copied verbatim, `send_mode=false`, migration `20260908120100` via `execute_sql` per #373) replace bodies that would otherwise say *"we have received your payment"* when nothing has been debited (#468 again), and three "client paid" bells re-word. A new `payment_intent.processing` block clears the stamp, `succeeded` belt-clears it, and **`payment_intent.canceled` — which is what micro-deposit expiry actually emits, NOT `payment_failed` — was widened off SpecRev into the failure block** for MAP 1 / TAX shapes — **too far, see the v822 hotfix at the end of this entry: on the generic first-payment arm a `canceled` now fails a row ONLY when that row is already `'processing'`.** **VERIFIED LIVE** on test client 62 (sandbox, fixtures wiped — tax plan 185 + 5 `client_tax_progress` rows + `pipeline_map1` 149; and the **bolded first sentence confirmed rendering bold** on Stripe's page. **A gap the implementing agent caught in review and fixed:** a P1 PaymentIntent carries `metadata.payment_number='1'`, and `isOffSession` is `!!md.payment_number`, so a cancelled Holistic P1 was being classified as an off-session installment and resolved to **nothing** — `isFirstInstallmentCancel` removes it for that event only (a P1 *bounce* is still deliberately left to `checkout.session.async_payment_failed`, same bell title, dedupes). **Owed, and it is most of the new code:** (1) ~~the `payment_intent.processing` clear has never fired~~ **FIRED 2026-10-13 in SANDBOX** (Tax retainer, plan 252, CHANGELOG 2026-10-13 (e)) once Jake subscribed the VFO Services endpoints that day (#614) — a LIVE `account: vfos` log line is still owed as the live proof; Exposure window: **exactly one day** — before v815-v817 this event was ignored for TAX / MAP 1 entirely. ## SYSTEM MAP

- **Frontend** `vfo-react` — `github.com/JLathamERT/vfo-portal`, local `C:\vfo-react`, live **https://vfoportal.com/**. Vite + React 18 + react-router-dom v6, `gh-pages` deploy. No backend code, no tests, no CI. **Architecture docs live in `vfo-react/docs/`** — in the *frontend* repo, documenting the backend.
- **Backend** `vfo-edge-functions` — `github.com/JLathamERT/vfo-edge-functions`, local `C:\vfo-edge-functions`. Deno 2 / Supabase Edge Runtime.
- **Supabase** project `ejpsprsmhpufwogbmxjv` ("VFO Showroom"), us-east-2, Postgres 17.
- **`vfo-admin-api`** — `index.ts` orchestrator + modular handler files in `actions/<group>/*.ts` + `router/dispatch.ts` + `router/webhooks.ts` + `middleware/auth.ts` + `utils/` + `constants/` + `integrations/`. Serves all 617 actions (derive with step 3). *(v: 2026-10-27)*
- **`boldsign-webhook`** — standalone. **Deploy with `--no-verify-jwt` (mandatory)** and **only with explicit approval** (#10, #180). **Helper functions** — `draft-agreement-pdfs` (on-demand Gmail drafts, no business logic) and `boldsign-template-fields` (a throwaway that reads a BoldSign template's field coordinates back for `agreement_templates.field_map`; `verify_jwt=true`, no business logic, safe to delete) — versions derived in step 1, never written here. *(v: 2026-09-14)*
- **Key entry points** — `index.ts` (logins) · `router/dispatch.ts` (PUBLIC_HANDLERS + AUTH_HANDLERS) · `router/webhooks.ts` (Stripe + BoldSign by shape) · `middleware/auth.ts` (six login types, role precedence) · `constants/role-gates.ts` (every gate list) · `utils/notify.ts` (all bells + the business-day helpers).
- **Storage buckets** — documented in [integrations/supabase.md](integrations/supabase.md); per-bucket prose preserved in [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md) under the 2026-08-14 restructure heading. **Public:** `tax-agreements`, `map1-agreements`, `map1-assets`, `advisor-onboarding-agreements`, `accountant-onboarding-agreements`, `specialist-onboarding-assets`, `vfo-widget`, `headshots`. **Private:** `specialist-dd-materials`, `client-tax-returns`, `client-documents`, `specialist-documents`, `specialist-tax-returns`, `member-vault`, `member-tax-returns`, `tax-planner-documents`, `member-ert-docs`, `client-ert-docs`, `specialist-ert-docs`, `presentation-templates`, `faq-documents` (2026-09-15), `specialist-showroom-docs` (2026-10-05 — the specialist vault's Showroom Documents, readable by MEMBERS + admins through `showroom_vault_download` only). *(v: 2026-10-05)*
- **NOT in the system:** no automated tests, no test runner, no CI/CD on either repo, no frontend TypeScript, no Supabase Auth (custom session tokens over `admin_sessions`, six login types / five portals, salted PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256), no React Context (just a promise cache + token-keyed last-seen snapshots in `src/lib/api.js`, #579), no structured logging (frontend errors go to Sentry only). **Email is Gmail DRAFTS by default** — only **seventy-one** `email_templates` rows have `send_mode=true` (#325; derive it with `select count(*) from email_templates where send_mode`, the ladder of past values is in CHANGELOG) *(v: 2026-10-27 — DERIVED 71: + 332 `TAX_decision_code` and the fifteen Tax Diagnostic rows, CHANGELOG 2026-10-27 (d); 2026-10-07 — template 310 `TAX_deposit_received|lead` was Draft then; 2026-10-05 — template 309 `manual_connect_payment` is Draft by Jake's call; 2026-10-02 — unmoved (the four `TRAINING` templates are Draft; the font pass changed bodies, not modes); no branch since 2026-09-24 writes `send_mode`, so the rises are Email Editor flips — which ones is not recoverable (no history column); #402 — derive it, never write it)*. Full prose → [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md).

## CURATED ALWAYS-APPLIES GOTCHAS

Money, auth/security, data-loss and cross-repo contracts only. **Everything narrower still lives in full in [GOTCHAS.md](GOTCHAS.md)** — nothing was deleted, and a `see #N` resolves there. When working an area, grep GOTCHAS.md for that area's keyword.

**Deploy / cross-repo**
- **#337 / #288** — a worktree can be cut from stale local main **and the freshness check can return a false 0**; run `git fetch origin && git rev-list --count HEAD..origin/main` → must be **0**, immediately before EVERY deploy, in the SAME invocation — and that invocation must `cd` **explicitly**, because the tool shell's cwd PERSISTS across calls and silently lands a repo-specific command in the other repo (#419). A stale edge worktree DEPLOYS REVERTED CODE over live. **#604** — the live function may be ANOTHER chat's deploy: read `list_edge_functions` `entrypoint_path` before testing a deploy, and merge origin/main before deploying over it *(v: 2026-10-12)*.
- **#376** — derive deploy state from git tags + MCP FIRST, then read docs; when they disagree git wins. Runs in BOTH directions — a "still owed" claim needs the same proof as a "done" claim (two hub entries claimed an undeployed frontend on 2026-09-02 while their `live-*` tags existed). **#464** — the dev server has **no `VITE_API_URL` override** and hits the LIVE function, so on a branch that changes a derivation on BOTH sides of the repo boundary the form previews the NEW rule while the server still writes the OLD one: `200` back, right numbers on screen, wrong numbers in the row. **Deploy the backend FIRST, then click through, then read the ROW** — a green preview is evidence about the FE copy and nothing else. **#543 is the REFUSAL twin:** a backend guard that only a NEW UI control can satisfy strands live users until the frontend ships — publish both in one sitting, or census the rows it will catch first. **#564 is the ANSWER twin:** a new server response the published page cannot follow strands live visitors between the two deploys — gate it on a flag only the new page sends (`pay_first`) *(v: 2026-09-30)*.
- **#145** — production deploys need an EXPLICIT "deploy"; "continue"/"yes" are not approval. **#36** — `docs/` exist in main AND every worktree; only ever edit the worktree copy.
- **#196** — every migration applied via MCP `apply_migration` MUST also be committed as a file, or a rebuilt environment silently lacks it. **#223** — multi-statement `execute_sql` returns only the LAST result.
- **#10** — `boldsign-webhook` deploys need `--no-verify-jwt` every time. **#74/#112** — use `deno check --no-lock`; a v5 `deno.lock` breaks the bundler. **#574** — `vfo-admin-api` is over Supabase's **5 MB server-side** deploy limit (plain and `--use-api` both 413): deploy with Docker Desktop running, `deno.lock` deleted, `--use-docker` (local bundle ~2 MB, 20 MB cap) *(v: 2026-10-02)*. **#134** — one angle bracket of JSX makes it a `.jsx` file, or the rollup build fails. **#545** — a whole-line delete on a CRLF file can fuse two lines: re-run the action count and scan for bare `\r` after it. **#578 / #580** — a portal route GitHub Pages does not hold as a file is a 404 + a second page load (emit it; GH Pages then serves `/admin/`, `main.jsx` strips the slash), and `gh-pages` deletes the old chunks under open tabs (`lazyPage` reloads once); test a production build with `vite preview --port 5174` (CORS allows 5173 – 5176, `constants/allowed-origins.ts`) *(v: 2026-10-02)*; **#608** — so an unmount cleanup that resets global state (the theme) is painted during the next lazy page's gap: derive it from the URL being navigated to *(v: 2026-10-12)*. **#599** — a login-guarded page renders NOTHING until its guard has redirected: a header child (NotificationBell) calling the API logged-out triggers `api.js`'s 401 hard-reload to `/` and loses an emailed deep link; test a live fix from a cold cache (GH Pages lets browsers cache `/` ~10 min) *(v: 2026-10-09)*. **#632** — a client NAME always opens the profile and the ROW opens the plan (`ClientNameLink` takes no `tab` / `plan`); a link that names no program is resolved by the PAGE (tab → program, `?plan=` → its own program), so stored bells stay right — never fix a link class only at its builders *(v: 2026-10-19)*. **#639** — `body { overflow-x: hidden }` makes body a never-scrolling scroll container, so every `position: sticky` (the portal header + nav bar, the bottom Save bars) stuck to it and scrolled away; `index.css` now says `overflow-x: clip` — never put `overflow: hidden/auto` on an ancestor of a sticky element *(v: 2026-10-20)*. **#676** — the same clip cuts off any row wider than the window with no scroll (the member Vault tab): a control row must wrap or collapse — tab bars through `CollapsibleNavBar`, the top bar through `PortalHeader`'s compact mode, both measured, never a fixed breakpoint *(v: 2026-10-27)*.
- **#401 / #402** — this hub is hard-capped at 250 lines and DERIVES volatile facts; every edit is declared ADD/UPDATE/DELETE/NOOP and superseded values move to CHANGELOG the same pass. **Never write a COUNT into prose** (rules, tables, columns, buckets) — write the query; a file contradicting itself on a quantity means nobody has read it end to end.
- **#399 / #436** — when a doc and a code comment disagree about sweep behaviour, the **handler wins**, and **a guard a doc asserts is not thereby implemented** — grep for the check before relying on it (07-server-chains claimed the licence processor's $0-stub skip for two months while the code had none); deleting a timed automation means grepping the docs tree for its interval AND vocabulary (`24h`, `auto-lock`, `auto-charge`) in the same commit.
- **#410 / #458** — **a provider's artifact is not evidence the artifact exists or is what the code emits.** A generated deck or bucket object is shared-writable and may have been hand-edited since, so generate a fresh one or read the handler (two agreeing samples from one sitting are ONE event). And **BoldSign `document/send` returning `200` + a documentId is NOT proof the document exists** — it can vanish asynchronously, after which every `getEmbeddedSignLink` 403s permanently, indistinguishable from the normal seconds-after-create race; the email then ships the red placeholder and `agreement_sent='Yes'` records success with no alarm. **A 403 loop that exhausts its budget should be suspected of the DOCUMENT, not the timing.** Both `tax/send-agreement.ts` and MAP 1's `contract-send-agreement.ts` carry the loop and the silent failure. **#535 / #536** — a PDF used to place BoldSign fields must come from the SAME render options as the send (`agreement_pdf_draft` lacked Letter/margin 0 until v887), and `agreement_templates`' unique index ignores `active`: stage a new agreement row under another `service_level` and swap in one transaction. **#567 is the return-URL twin:** a provider sends people to a `return_url` whenever they LEAVE its hosted flow, finished or not, so `/payout-setup?done=1` told Lance Anderson he was done four times — read the provider's real state (`connect_setup_status`) before any success copy *(v: 2026-10-01)*. **#610** — the same edge for OUR return URL: a Checkout reachable from an EMAILED link returns to the token page's own thank-you, never a page that needs a login (route A's portal return put a member on the login screen) *(v: 2026-10-13)*. **#583** — a re-fetched signer link (BoldSign embedded link) must name the signer the SEND registered — the MEMBER on member-pays — with the same sandbox key; a sandbox run hides the mismatch by construction *(v: 2026-10-05)*.

**Auth / security**
- **#141 / #142 / #143** — see the SECURITY INVARIANTS box above; they are the same four rules. **#672 / #673** — an emailed button that charges, refunds or releases a share logs a decision ONLY via the code mailed To its recipient (`DecisionCodeCard` + `consumeDecisionCode`); a PUBLIC handler that moves money refuses all but the service-role bearer (`serviceRoleRefusal`) — PUBLIC registration is for the chain, not the world.
- **#455 / #490** — **a gate list only guards the body key it constrains.** `MEMBER_SCOPED_ACTIONS` does ONE thing: rewrite `body.member_number`. A handler keyed on a DIFFERENT body id (`enrollment_id`, `client_id`, `ciq_id`) is therefore **completely unguarded while looking gated** — `msm_load_clients` returned any member's whole client list off a sequential enrollment id for months. **Read the handler and name the field it filters on; never reason from a list's NAME.** The mirror of #309, and the reason the member vault actions sit in NO list *on purpose*, confined by `denyIfNotOwnClient` as the first statement of each. **#490 is the write-side rule: hand a role self-service by adding a SECOND, NARROWER HANDLER, never by widening the admin handler's gate** — the admin writer carries admin-only powers (notes, escape statuses, any-row access) and one gate move hands over all of them at once, so `training_member_save_task` was built beside an untouched `msm_save_training_task`. **And every admin-only ESCAPE VALUE must be refused TWICE — as an INPUT and as the EXISTING value:** stripping `Stopped`/`N/A` from the options a member may send is only half of it, and without the second check a member silently un-stops a plan an MSM deliberately stopped. A UI lock (a greyed chip, a filtered dropdown) is cosmetic; the handler refusal is the guard. Corollary: when the new handler needs a rule the frontend already owns, that rule becomes a **cross-repo contract** (`NOT_DONE_STATUSES` now lives in both repos and must stay byte-identical — the #339 family). **#495 is the tab-list mirror:** the per-tab gate applies to EVERY non-superadmin caller, members included, so a read a member surface fires must never sit under a `TAB_ACTIONS` key (it 403s exactly the members it serves) — a tab key holds the tab's writers; a member-callable read sits in no list, commented, confined in-handler. **#511 is both rules at once: a guard written for role X is not a guard for role Y** — `denyIfNotPlannerClient` returns `null` for every non-planner, so three tax loaders that "had a guard" let any member read any client's plans by id, while five tax WRITES sat in no list at all; for every action a role can reach, name the guard that fences THAT role, and prove the list with a probe script (`scripts/probe-tax-intake-refusals.ps1`). **#515 is the same hole on a CHAIN TARGET:** `automation_TAX_sendagreement` was reached only over HTTP by three handlers and sat in NO list, so any member could send an agreement on any plan — every name a chain's `action:` field can produce is gated like a button, and when a new ROLE gains a chain SOURCE the target's gate must admit that role's token on the second hop (the planner's Direct-only widening added all four names together, fenced in-handler by `denyIfPlannerOnClassicPlan`). **#546 is the PUBLIC-source case:** a token-link handler has no session, so it cannot chain an AUTH action at all — chain to a service-role-ONLY public twin (`automation_TAX_sendagreement_system`: exact service-role bearer or 403, no session fallback) and re-read the target's column afterwards; never widen the AUTH action *(v: 2026-09-25)*. **#537 is the other half of #490:** once a narrow writer owns a column, the WIDE admin writer that echoes the loaded row must stop writing it (`member_profile_save` deletes the branding columns), or an open admin form reverts the member's change. **#600** — an action in NO list whose handler takes no `auth` is member-callable, and a DEAD name in a gate list makes the list look complete (22 such writers, v959): census `dispatch.ts` for handlers not passed `c.auth`. **#601** — never widen a shared fence: `denyIfNotOwnPlan` IS `denyIfNotOwnClient` one hop later and fences every tax write, so a second reader gets its own gate (`mirrorReadGate`) opted in by name on reads only. **#602** — masking money for a viewer keeps the KEYS (screens read amounts for shape: `final_retainer_amount != null` = 3-payment) and the key-name rule is subtractive — a new money column must carry a mask word *(v: 2026-10-12)*; **#669** — the match is by SUBSTRING (`value`, `link`, `url`…), so a non-money key added to a mirrored read can be masked too *(v: 2026-10-26)*.
- **#655** — a resent or copied set-up link is a password-reset key until it expires: using ANY link expires the others (`submit_login_setup`, 2026-10-22); a new credential-link minter must say what happens to the old link. **#677** — `member_logins.email` is UNIQUE: a set-up link for a person's second member number fails at the INSERT while the old row owns the login — re-point the login row instead, then census what the old number owns *(v: 2026-10-27)*. **#309** — every `*_upload_url` signed-URL minter must be provably covered by a NAMED gate; "in no gate list" means UNGATED, not safe. **#310** — for a PUBLIC token page the token ROW is the whole credential: bucket + path derive server-side, the body selects nothing, ever; **#548** — a once-per-EMAIL action on a durable token needs a per-email discriminator on the link (`q=<count>`), not a credential. **#549** — every upload surface must run the SAME post-PUT notifier as the others; a surface that bells only at mint time skips every "document arrived" stamp and ask *(v: 2026-09-25)*. **#570** — a token page may let the visitor CHOOSE only among the token row's OWN entity's sections (a vault drop link, `section='any'`); a fixed-section row ignores the body, and bucket + path still derive from the row *(v: 2026-10-01)*. **#531 / #532** — a PUBLIC action with NO token rate-limits on the platform-stamped IP (`cf-connecting-ip`), never the caller-written first `X-Forwarded-For` entry, and never in `login_attempts`; it never emails an address the form typed (spam relay), and strips CR/LF/`<>` from every single-line answer at the WRITE, because downstream Subjects and PDFs read the stored value. **#586** — a store OUTSIDERS can read is its own bucket, written only with a server-side `acknowledge_public` and never by a public token page, read through an action that cannot name any other store (the specialist Showroom Documents) *(v: 2026-10-05)*.
- **#257 / #467** — `TAX_PLANNER_ALLOWED_ACTIONS` is the ONLY boundary for a planner caller, so every planner-callable action ALSO needs an in-handler group-scope guard; the entire token-forwarding chain closure must be allowlisted + guarded. **#467 is the pair of traps that sit either side of a widening:** an allowlist entry is INERT while an admin-only step GATES the step it grants (read the step ORDER, not just the gate lists — `Client decision 1` was planner-allowed for months and unreachable), and a handler that has only ever served ONE role has NO ownership guard, so widening a deny-by-default role onto it is an instant IDOR unless you add one in the same change. Widen the write PATH too, not just the action: if the step also calls `tax_save_task`, exercise the answer that writes NOTHING — the one that writes money self-heals and hides the gap. **#581** — a BATCHED loader re-applies each original call's PER-ITEM fence: `tax_load_plans include_details` attaches details only to plans the planner's group holds, because the plan-level rule is stricter than the client-level one *(v: 2026-10-02)*.
- **#577** — a ledger a member can read (`gc_transactions`, `select("*")`) stores its actor as a NAME, never an email *(v: 2026-10-02)*. **#445 / #207** — the two big payloads redact in OPPOSITE directions: members is an ADDITIVE whitelist (a new column is private until you list it), **`experts` is `select("*")` + SUBTRACTIVE per-caller redaction — a new column is PUBLIC until you strip it, at all THREE sites** (`data/load-data.ts` non-admin branch, `data/specialist-showroom-load.ts`, `data/client-showroom-load.ts`). Miss one and the column leaks silently to every non-admin caller. The narrow `experts` anon column grant is a SECOND, independent list a new column never joins — check both, and never assume the direction from the members precedent. **#664** — the client showroom also has a client-ONLY strip list (`stripClientHiddenDetails` ↔ FE `CLIENT_HIDDEN_FIELDS`, today `D&B_getting_started`) *(v: 2026-10-23)*.
- **#385** — an allowlist entry is NOT the grant: **before widening any allowlist, grep the target handler for a second in-handler gate** (`isTaxAdmin`, `isSuperadmin`, a bare email compare). Inverse of #309.
- **#256** — a gate entry matching no dispatched action protects nothing; grep `dispatch.ts` for the EXACT registered name. **#167** — a new action behind a tab-gated surface must join `TAB_ACTIONS[...]` or granted admins 403.
- **#338** — the `accounting` grant is the WHOLE boundary for every control in the tab, money included; do NOT restore an in-handler superadmin check. **#353** — the add-member restriction is FRONTEND-ONLY: a guardrail, not a boundary.
- **#355** — `request_password_reset` is anti-enumeration by construction (always `{success:true}`, 1200 ms floor, throttle counts every request); never add a distinguishing branch or widen the response. **#144** — new login-type actions must join `LOGIN_ACTIONS`. **#259** — a new restricted role that allowlists the bell must scope BOTH load and mark-read to email, or admin broadcasts leak.
- **#425 / #426** — **a session's role comes from `admin_sessions.login_type`, the portal that minted it, re-verified against that login table (row gone ⇒ 401, never a fall-through).** One email may hold several portal identities; each session is locked to one. **NULL `login_type` = a legacy session on the old email-precedence fallback.** **Every new login action MUST stamp `login_type`** (pairs with #144's `LOGIN_ACTIONS` rule) or it silently drops onto that fallback. `api.js` still redirects on 401 only and never acts on a 403 — the portal shells now banner on a failed startup load, but when a surface is blank and the data exists, read `function_edge_logs` for 403s FIRST.
- **#11** — a handler chaining another AUTH handler must forward `body.token`, not just the header.
- **#448** — **a predicate is only as good as the SELECT feeding it.** A discriminator column missing from an explicit `.select(...)` makes the property `undefined`, so the predicate is silently, permanently FALSE — no error, no missing-field symptom, because the false branch is the pre-existing legacy behaviour. Three handlers shipped that way in one day. A shared predicate's columns are part of its CONTRACT: name them at the predicate, and audit EVERY explicit-list caller in the same change (`select("*")` callers are safe). **#451 is the defensive half:** when you add an optional column to a SHARED RESOLVER's input type, make it `T | null | undefined` and branch on all three — `undefined` ("the caller never told me") triggers a lookup, `null` ("there is none") does not. A plain `?: T | null` collapses those two and turns every un-audited call site into a silent permanent bug; the three-state read is what makes the audit a nicety instead of load-bearing.

**Money**
- **#485** — **a webhook verifier that tries several secrets must RECORD which one matched: that match is the ONLY evidence of which Stripe account an event came from.** And because a Stripe customer / payment-method / PaymentIntent id is valid on **exactly one account**, a global key switch strands every saved customer — so the account is a property of the ROW, not the deployment: **CONFIG MINTS** (`pipeline_sandbox_config.stripe_account` decides where a NEW customer/session is created, nothing else) and **THE ROW STAMP GOVERNS** (nullable `stripe_account`, NULL = legacy = `vfos`, read by every later call, re-stamped by exactly one supervised action after the provider's own customer copy). Corollaries: log the account on every event and warn on livemode-vs-secret disagreement; a mode guard becomes a mode-AND-account guard; make single-account pipelines **inert** for a foreign event (`fromPrimary`), not merely wrong; a "primary" row filter needs `is.null` OR `eq.vfos`, never a plain `.eq()`. **#487 / #319** — **`timeoutFor()`'s 60 s tier is keyed on the action NAME (`automation_*`), not on the work**, so any other long-running write silently gets the 20 s tier — and the client giving up never stops the handler: design every bulk action to be idempotent and re-runnable, and read the DB, not the browser, after a timeout.
- **#252** — a revshare `share` is ALWAYS dollars of the TOTAL engagement; portion = `(share / totalGross) × paymentReceived`. Payout and display must use the same math. **#659 / #661** — every split reads ONE helper (`revshareBaseFor`), a base that can move after the money does is stamped at the payout, and a per-plan money switch leaves switch-off plans byte-identical (prove by replay); **#660** — a refund button claims the row and reads the provider's existing refunds before making one (a key replays 24 h only) *(v: 2026-10-23)*. **#670** — `clients.status='lost'` is a MONEY GATE: every client charge path and pay link calls `isClientLost` / `lostClientIds` (`utils/client-lost.ts`) and refuses, failing closed on a read error; a new one must too *(v: 2026-10-26)*.
- **#394** — the three legs are NOT prorated alike: MEMBER pays its entered share in full, STRATEGIC is gross-prorated, VFOS is the RESIDUAL. **Copy `contract-revshare.ts`; never re-derive from column names.** **#465** — a cell rendered as THE RESIDUAL launders every payee the component has never heard of into itself and still sums to its header, so **enumerate payees from the payout engine, never from the columns already rendered**; `Math.max(residual, 0)` then converts an over-booked row into a plausible `$0.00`, eating the one exact assertion that the parts agree with the whole. **#468(b) is its inverse and the pair is the test:** a residual that SWALLOWS a payee still sums to its header (a wrong breakdown with a right total), while an OMITTED payee under-sums and announces itself — the Payments table named three of Bitzer’s four legs and reached $1,215 of a $1,350 installment. So when a payout engine gains a leg, grep every surface that RENDERS the split, not only those that COMPUTE it, and assert legs === row total. Where several surfaces prorate the same money, one being right says nothing about the others — collapse them onto one helper that quotes the engine, fallback included, and prove a "legacy" branch dead with a census before porting it. **#466** — a SECOND WRITER reusing an existing type value conflates the two events permanently and every reader inherits it (`gc_add_credits` writes `"purchased"` for a comp): give a new writer its OWN value, and where you cannot, copy the test used by whichever total was already right — it will be keyed on a field the event itself produces (`amount_usd`, `stripe_session_id`), never on the type. Two lists in two repos that must agree are the same failure with a longer fuse, and an admin action with no `created_by` answers no questions later.
- **#609** — widening a money-like column to `numeric`: every JS read that does `+` goes through one coercing helper (`gcCredits()`), or a string value concatenates silently *(v: 2026-10-13)*. **#304** — `member_contribution` is a member-funded DISCOUNT and Member Share is entered ALREADY NET of it — never subtract or scale it again.
- **#303** — NEVER initialise a payout-leg status to a TERMINAL value with the transfer behind a condition; use an exhaustive branch chain, and a non-terminal status is safe only if EVERY retry path accepts it.
- **#377** — `"Pending"` is a POST-TRIGGER value the sweep RETRIES; a backfill must write **NULL** for a leg that has not been triggered. **#380** — when a guard stops short-circuiting, re-audit every sibling leg's terminal check. **#441** — the same rule read from the other end: when a payout is DEFERRED to a later payment, the legs staying NULL is the whole mechanism, and the sweep's candidate scan may already be picking the row up on some *other* satisfied precondition (the tax final retainer: `retainer_receipt_number` is stamped at the INITIAL payment) — so only the retry-only gate keeps it from paying early. Never pre-stamp a deferred leg, and name which gate is actually holding it.
- **#265** — a stranded-leg rescue must verify the UPSTREAM leg resolved first. **#277** — a backfill of an already-collected payment MUST settle every leg its sweep can rescue. **#362** — a pre-settled leg is skipped FOREVER; check Stripe Transfers before assuming either way.
- **#335** — Payment Continuation is a SECOND write path into `pipeline_map1`/`client_tax_plans` and must carry the strategic split; a missing one is INVISIBLE (NULL reads as "no partner", nothing reports it).
- **#327** — Stripe REDELIVERS a webhook whose 200 we were too slow to return and there is NO event-id dedupe; name the column that proves a side effect already happened and check it first. **#470 is the same rule for a TIMESTAMP, which nobody defends:** a provider redelivers its terminal event, so an unconditional `col = now()` in that branch silently re-dates history on every redelivery — write `existing || now` (BoldSign re-stamped the CEO countersign date, invisibly, on the column the balance display gates on). **#473** — **when one row gains a SECOND collection on the SAME customer, every webhook branch keyed on the customer must split on a `payment_kind` carried on the SESSION metadata** (in `checkout.session.completed` the PaymentIntent has not been fetched yet, so the session is all you have — set the kind on BOTH), the new branch goes AHEAD of the old one and the old one keeps its own positive guard, and **every generic failure resolver must SKIP the new kinds** or it writes a real status into the neighbouring leg's column. Nothing here fails loudly. **#611** — a once-per-form email fired from a redelivered branch CLAIMS its latch before sending (`update … where col is null returning`) and releases it on a failed send; stamp-after-send lets two deliveries both send *(v: 2026-10-13)*. **#228** — off-session idempotency keys must be LOGICAL/date-less. **#15** — rotate keys per RETRY, never per logical charge.
- **#469** — **a column DEFAULT is a value a reader cannot distinguish from a written one** (not NULL, not zero, right type, plausible range), so a figure derived from it renders confidently wrong before anyone fills it in: `payment_amount DEFAULT 4000` printed *"nothing further due"* on unpriced onboardings. **Gate every derived figure on the column that proves the write happened** (here `agreement_signed_by_ceo_at`) and say so on screen; when you add such a default, name its "written for real" event in the same breath. **#478** — **a field that STORES a charge date must not be EDITED as one:** the member has already paid for the period they are in, so take *"when does it start"* as input, derive the renewal with the **same helper the sweep uses**, preview the derived date on screen, and refuse a start whose renewal is already past — a "next charge date" box invites a second deduction (a yearly GC sub was eight days from taking 10 more credits). **#429** — a NULL in an optional column can BE the switch that selects the OLD money behaviour (`charge_day` on a monthly transfer: set = save-only link + year scheduled on that day; NULL = the legacy pay-at-the-link charge). Nothing on screen says which mode a row is in, so a missed backfill or an omitted form key silently bills the wrong way — **prove such a backfill with a query, never a spot-check**, and when a column gains a SECOND writer, state which one owns it in which plan state. **#499** — to change ONE money outcome for ONE client, add a nullable column that ONE handler reads (capped at what was captured), never re-state the fee columns: whatever says "not yet collected" is what the collector reads on the other path.
- **#433 / #240** — **`members.suspended` / `paused` / `membership_arrears` GATE payouts, not just displays** (v778; `membership_arrears` replaced `membership_suspended` 2026-09-15 — arrears is its OWN reason with its own held value, never folded into "suspended"): every member payout engine must carry all three in its member `select` and hold the MEMBER leg only. **#498** — retire such a column by ADD + backfill → deploy → DROP, never a rename in place: the live function's select of the old name would read `undefined` = not held = the money moves. Adding a NON-TERMINAL status (2026-08-24 also `lic_payment_status='scheduled'` — a subscription with zero money moved, **#437**, where PostgREST `.neq` silently drops the NULL rows you need) means teaching **every** enumerating predicate by hand — engine `isResolved`, every sweep pickup list, every FE `PAYABLE`/`PENDING` set — or the leg strands silently; export the list from the engine and import it into the sweep. **#432 / #504** — **#251's client-keyed test-member force covers only client-keyed pipelines.** SpecRev's sandbox mode is the GLOBAL `"MAP 1"` toggle, so firing it on a member with a half-built account REAL-SENDS `SPECREV_connect_setup`; and MEMBERSHIP is **member-keyed**, which needed its own twin (`loadSandboxConfigForMember`, 2026-09-16). **A force must be applied at the SAME READ the mode guards use — never by stamping the row alone**, because every v619-style guard compares `plan.sandbox` against the pipeline toggle, so a hand-stamped row trips the mismatch bell and its charge is silently SKIPPED. Force-ON only, and for a sweep that reads the config ONCE, OR the check into each per-plan USE. **#568:** test a global-toggle pipeline (SpecRev = `MAP 1`) only AFTER the toggle is flipped AND read back — one step early, the already-paid path moved a real $10 to ERT and real-sent a connect email *(v: 2026-10-01)*.
- **#463** — **one constant plus a strict comparison defines exactly TWO bands.** Adding a third needs **two NAMED edges** (a floor and a ceiling, both strict, so every boundary total is answerable from the constants and not from someone's `>` vs `>=`) and **ONE shared, non-throwing core that every consumer imports** — pricing, the amend handler, the deck, the invoice labels, the FE mirror; enumerate them first, because the consumer that cannot tolerate a throw is what proves the range check belongs in the wrapper, not the core (#453). **A DISPLAY LABEL must be derived from the ARITHMETIC, not from the shape flag:** *"Retainer (50%)"* printed on every non-3-payment plan because "not 3-payment" had always meant "even split", and a lopsided 2-payment plan would have contradicted itself in writing to the client — test `retainer × 2 === total` in whole cents (1c tolerance). And **reuse an existing storage shape if one exists** rather than adding a column or a boolean, or the new band becomes a new branch in every consumer.
- **#287** — purchase-email policy: card = receipt only, ACH = confirmation + docs at settle, check = both at clear. Gate PLACEMENT is load-bearing. **#510 is placement as a POLICY, not a mechanism: a document must not reach the client before the decision it documents.** A revised invoice chained to the admin's decision EMAIL handed the client a corrected bill for a choice they had not yet made; it belongs on their own green CLICK, beside the confirmation. When a chain is "best-effort, fire it wherever it works", ask which side of the client's answer each artefact belongs on — the question and the answer are two different emails. **#584 — one event, one document:** the threaded revised invoice made the fresh-invoice-at-next-payment arms a duplicate, so later tax payments are receipt-only, amended or not; a new document chain retires the old one in the same change *(v: 2026-10-05)*. **#475 is the ACH policy's hard edge: WIDENING a payment method widens what "completed" MEANS.** Unpinning `verification_method` (#298) lets a client type account + routing numbers, and Stripe then fires `checkout.session.completed` at **SUBMIT** with the PaymentIntent in `requires_action` and **no debit attempted** — so **read the PI's own `status`; the session's arrival is not evidence of a payment**, and the confirmation body that says *"we have received your payment"* becomes a lie you send (#468). Record the sub-state in a **nullable SIDE-COLUMN, never a new status value** — a side-column is invisible to code that does not read it, so every settle branch, failure branch, sweep, resolver and FE status map needs zero changes, where a fourth status value means auditing all of them (#371). And **whenever you expose a path that can EXPIRE, find the event the expiry actually emits** — micro-deposit expiry is `payment_intent.canceled`, not `payment_failed`, so a row would otherwise sit at `processing` forever with no bell. Two traps ride along: a metadata field two producers now set no longer means what its original consumer assumed (`payment_number='1'` made the generic first-payment resolver skip a cancelled P1), and a template discriminator must join the handler's explicit `.select(...)` in the SAME edit or the predicate is silently, permanently false (#448). **#482 is the narrowing that widening needed, and it is the general rule: a CANCELED PaymentIntent is not a failed payment unless the row was BOOKED against it** — Stripe mints the PI when a Checkout page merely OPENS and cancels it (`automatic`, indistinguishable from an expired verification) 24h after that page expires unpaid, so `!== 'succeeded'` reads *absence* of a payment as evidence of a *failed* one; gate on `=== 'processing'`, the row's own booking (#431/#444's positive allowlist, on the write side), and remember that a terminal status on a column a durable client link reads is a write to the client's ACCESS, not just to a report (it killed Pat Hurst's `/tax-pay` link, 2026-09-09). **#491 is the SUBSCRIPTION-mode twin: any amount due today makes Stripe HIDE manual bank entry (the sub is `incomplete` and voided after 23h, micro-deposits take days), so a "collect this month now + schedule the rest" construct silently loses the #298 fallback; a $0-today session saves the bank through a SetupIntent that lives on NEITHER the session NOR the subscription (`GET /v1/setup_intents?customer=` is the only source, and that list is a HISTORY — scope it to `created >= session.created`), its events are `setup_intent.*` (which must be SUBSCRIBED), and they must resolve by the STORED intent id, never by customer, because every stale intent expires on its own clock.** **#607** — a document's fee wording gates on the fee AMOUNT (`cardFee > 0`), never the method, and a fee waiver (`card_fee_waived`, membership's New Model rule) is part of the charge rule at EVERY gross-up site, recovery links included *(v: 2026-10-12)*. **#665** — an emailed link is not a person: bots load token pages and CLICK Confirm, so no emailed button may move money on its own — it records a request a VFOS admin confirms *(v: 2026-10-23)*.
- **#282** — `allocateDocNumber` returns its candidate even when the reservation INSERT fails; verify the reservation landed or you issue duplicate accounting numbers. **#315** — a multi-period collection must change a row's AMOUNT, never the row COUNT. **#533** — an ACH payment that CREATES records (a client, a plan, anything a transfer keys on) creates them at `payment_intent.succeeded`, never at `checkout.session.completed` (the PI is only `processing`/`requires_action` there); re-minting a Checkout must CONFIRM the old session closed (an erroring `/expire` is UNKNOWN → refuse), and copy the verified precedents (Background Check request + Tax retainer) branch for branch.
- **#454** — **copying an id to route the money silently re-points every OTHER surface that reads it.** A corporate member borrows their lead's `stripe_account_id` by a straight copy (`stripe_account_linked_from` is a MARKER — the four payout engines were unchanged, the copy IS the routing), and that same copy turned an admin setup email AND the durable public `/payout-setup` token into onboarding links **into the lead's Stripe account**, and the by-`destination` payout listing into a leak of the lead's and every sibling's history. **Before making one row borrow another's identifier, enumerate every reader of the borrowed column and split them into "meant the value" and "meant WHOSE it is"** — the second group is all wrong, and none of it fails a build, a type-check or a click-through of the feature you just built. **#592** — the owning member is `clients.member_number`, never the client_ref PREFIX (a corporate number has a dash in it: MAP 1 paid the LEAD for `58088-FC6-001` until v947) *(v: 2026-10-07)*.
- **#159 / #317 / #268 / #479** — verify `capabilities.transfers === 'active'` before a Connect transfer — **an account id is NOT a payable account** (the admin's "Set Up Payment Details" button creates the Express account on the click, so `stripe_account_id` is set long before the member can receive anything), so probe it from ONE shared helper that never throws, park a half-built account **non-terminally** where the sweep already retries it, and raise the held bell + the member's cumulative setup email ONCE per new hold (`member_held_shares`, #625 — 2026-10-15), never per retry tick, clearing the stale FAILED bell. Before copying "park it, the sweep retries" into a neighbouring engine, read that engine's sweep and name the status it picks up (PIP's `Pending` is not swept, which is why PIP was left alone). every account link must append `collection_options[fields]=eventually_due` (else a silent $3,000 cap); never email a raw `connect.stripe.com` link — use the durable `/payout-setup?token=` page. **#488** — a Connect transfer flows **platform → its own connected account ONLY**, so a second company's share must be collected on the PLATFORM and FORWARDED, never collected on that company's own account (that strands every member leg, which can only be paid from the platform balance); connecting an existing standalone account is **OAuth, not account creation** (the returned `stripe_user_id` IS the existing account), and its id is a **destination, never a key**; and **two legs on one row need two independent status columns AND two independent candidate queries** — a line terminal for the member can still owe the other leg, so the second leg gets its own loop, its own sweep query and its own counters. **#489** — a fixed `Idempotency-Key` replays Stripe's FIRST response for **24 h including an ERROR body**, so one environmental refusal locks the retry out until tomorrow (the tell is an identical `request_log_url`): **probe before you spend a key**, and rotate the key only on a status your code writes **after a confirmed error** (a network drop leaves the ambiguous case on the ORIGINAL key, which is correct). **Stripe does NOT copy a transfer's description/metadata to the destination payment** — stamp it yourself on the connected account, best-effort, never gating payout status.
- **#626 / #627 / #630** — a Stripe SUBSCRIPTION started through Checkout sits on an INACTIVE `product_data` product (re-price onto a product you own) and is PINNED to the payment-method type it was paid with (re-pin `payment_settings.payment_method_types` when moving it), and its `invoice.paid` can beat `checkout.session.completed` — resolve by the stored subscription id, then the subscription's own metadata row id, claim the month on a unique invoice id, and never route a multi-subscription customer by customer *(v: 2026-10-16)*.
- **#396 / #397 / #552** — reminder ladders count BUSINESS days via `businessDelayCutoffIso`; it walks whole weekdays FIRST then subtracts fractional hours, and **a bigger `days` must always yield an earlier-or-equal cutoff**. The calendar survivors are deliberate — see [architecture/07-server-chains.md](architecture/07-server-chains.md). **Counting decides whether a reminder is DUE, never WHEN it may go (#552):** every reminder PASS checks `reminderWindowOpen` (Mon–Fri 08:00–17:59 ET) and skips query + send + stamp when closed — never gate in the email layer (failed sends stamp too) — and a sweep carrying a ladder must be scheduled 13:00–14:59 UTC. *(v: 2026-09-26)*

**Data loss**
- **#206** — Supabase blocks direct SQL `DELETE FROM storage.objects`; delete vault files through the Storage API. **#305** — `client_tax_progress.tax_specialist_id` is FK ON DELETE SET NULL and NULL means PLAN-LEVEL, so a naive specialist delete orphans progress rows and corrupts plan state.
- **#187** — ALWAYS destructure `{ data, error }` in webhook/automation code; otherwise a real DB failure is indistinguishable from "no rows" and fails 100% silently. **#427** is the same family one level up: after calling anything that UPDATES a row you are holding, your copy is STALE — re-read before passing it to any consumer keyed on the mutated column (a stale key filters to zero rows just as silently, and "fix the filter" breaks the money math instead). **#215** — multi-row inserts fill missing keys with NULL, not the column default. **#493** — two data-modifying CTEs in ONE statement that hit the SAME row: the second silently no-ops and its `RETURNING` just omits the row — one row, one statement, and verify by re-reading the row, not by the returned count (pairs with #223).
- **#431 / #444** — a status vocabulary in a migration comment is a SNAPSHOT, not a constraint (these columns are bare `text` with no CHECK). Before gating a DESTRUCTIVE action on a status, enumerate the values from every writer of that column, use a positive allowlist not a denylist, and back it with a column that is directly true of the thing you care about (a non-null PaymentIntent id beats any status string). **The money-side corollary: a status DENYLIST in a CHARGE sweep charges every value it has never heard of** — the 2026-08-26 cancel button would have had the nightly MAP 1 sweep charging cancelled installments, because `skipStatuses` predated `'cancelled'`. **Invert to a positive charge-only-on-EMPTY test BEFORE introducing any new terminal status**, and read an unknown status as "someone else owns this slot", never as "charge it".

**Cross-repo contracts**
- **#339 / #460** — the tax step machine (now `utils/tax-plan-steps.ts`, called by `overview-tax.ts` AND the planner portal) is the BACKEND MIRROR of the FE `isTaskStatused`; add both branches in the same change, and remember `computeTrack` is POSITIONAL — warnings, `next_action` and `next_actions` are all a function of ORDER. **#460 is the same failure INSIDE one component, which no cross-repo check catches:** the allocation step has THREE done-tests — the step's own dot (either slot), `isTaskStatused` (planner slot ONLY, and it drives the phase pills + hero counts), and the backend catch-all (progress row, so either slot) — and on a team-member-only plan they disagree. When you split a column, enumerate every done-test of that step; the one driving the visible dot is rarely the one driving the counts. **#544** — a SYNTHETIC step pushed into that machine must count as DONE whenever the real step after it is done, or every historic plan grows a warning. **#560** — the plan LIST label (`getPlanState`) is a THIRD copy of the done-rules and must match the hero count (it once required a plan-wide Tax 6 row, so no plan could read Completed). *(v: 2026-09-29)* **#569 — a LEGACY-migrated row proves a step somewhere else** (a progress row, a paid retainer, a received-at stamp): the step machine's done-tests now accept it, after Marc Ehrhardt's migrated plan read Completed on its page and Live everywhere else — run `buildTaxPlanSteps` on the disputed row before deciding which copy is wrong *(v: 2026-10-01)*. **#571** — the FE done-rules (`isTaskStatused`, exclusions, phase states, hero counts) now live in ONE module, `src/components/admin/tax/taxPlanRules.js` `makeTaxPlanRules`, shared by the plan page and the planner Clients Progress column: any surface summarising a plan CALLS it over the plan page's own inputs, never re-derives them (`getPlanState` is the remaining copy). **#572** — the intake's `parentId` follow-ups are a cross-repo contract (both question-list copies validate + blank them), and question NUMBERS are display-only: every view numbering answers goes through `numberTaxIntakeQuestions` *(v: 2026-10-01)*; **#642** — a changed question gets a NEW id and the old one `retired` (stored answers are labelled from the current list), and a changed radio option needs a legacy map in the normaliser or an open old page 400s *(v: 2026-10-20)*. **#573** — a phase note keyed only by (client, phase, tab) LEAKS into every sibling track with the same phase names: a multi-instance track passes `scope` to `PhaseNotesPanel` and counts with `noteInScope` (Regular + Tax done 2026-10-02; PIP Meetings still shares) *(v: 2026-10-02)*. **#576** — Tax 6 step 3 (*Specialist confirms VFOS Gross Rev*) is VFOS's (the member's on Direct) unless the block's specialist is the group's `linked_expert_id`; step 4 (*PC confirms receipt of VFOS Gross Rev*) is VFOS's under EVERY specialist (`TAX6_ALWAYS_VFOS_STEP_NAMES`, 2026-10-20): the names live in BOTH repos (`utils/tax6-ownership.ts` `isTax6VfosOwned` / `TaxPrioritiesTab.jsx` `isTax6VfosStep`), the boundary is save-task + direct-save-task, the chip reads `group_linked` *(v: 2026-10-20)*. **#640** — a Skip that satisfies a step is a NEW column beside its done-stamp, and every reader of the stamp reads both (book-ends: `generated_bookends_skipped_at` in the step machine, save-task's Detailed gate, the meeting-confirm bell, the Tax 4 sweep wording, the tab lock) — grep the column, not the step name *(v: 2026-10-20)*. **#594** — the onboarding step locks are the same contract: `onboardingOrder.js` draws them, `utils/onboarding-order.ts` refuses them, the step LABELS are the hint text *(v: 2026-10-07)*. **#635 / #636** — name / phone tidying + the one-person-per-name rule live in BOTH `utils/person-format.ts` and `src/lib/formatters.js` (tidy BEFORE comparing); the client duplicate key is the EMAIL portal-wide (`client_logins.email` is unique), and a writer fed RESENT values (`msm_update_client` from the status / PF saves) judges only a CHANGE *(v: 2026-10-19)*. **#637** — `finalizeTaxIntake` reuses the member's own same-email client and whatever `client_id` / `tax_plan_id` the intake already carries: pre-linking a paid intake by hand means stamping BOTH. **#638** — every `Subject:` is ASCII on the wire: the delivery layer (`encodeSubjectMime` beside the footer guard) encodes it, so a sender that bypasses `gmailDraftFetch` / `deliverRaw` bypasses both *(v: 2026-10-19)*. **#647 / #648 / #649** — member contacts trigger on the MEMBER role only (`WORK_ROLE_TOKENS` never), a money email that does not read `member_paying_on_behalf` leaks to the client on a member-pays plan, and the member-pays split is a server preset in all six split writers *(v: 2026-10-21)*.
- **#414** — a plan MIGRATED from the legacy system never got its branch column backfilled, so a step machine that keys a whole branch on that one column points every migrated row weeks into the past. **Prove a branch by the columns that branch itself writes** (agreement sent / signed / paid / decided), not by the decision field alone — and check for legacy rows before trusting any single-column branch test. **#508 is the guard-side twin: a precondition keyed on a status only the PORTAL writes refuses every legacy-migrated row, 100% of the time, and the refusal reads like a correct one.** `retainer_receipt_status !== 'Sent'` meant "no invoice was ever sent" on a portal plan and "this row came from the old system" on a migrated one; the legacy FINGERPRINT is the set of NULLs a portal write would have filled (here `retainer_receipt_status` + `retainer_invoice_drive_id` beside a populated legacy invoice number), so branch on the fingerprint and keep the original guard verbatim for everyone else.
- **#403** — a **terminal/negative** outcome can SATISFY a "cascade complete" predicate, so `locked: !cascadeDone` unlocks exactly the closed records. Test the decline/refund/stop state FIRST, and back the UI lock with a handler 400 — the lock alone is choreography (#353). **#620 / #621** — the reverse: when step B gates on step A, an answered B must make A FINAL (A's done-test true once B is answered, mirrored #339, AND a server refusal), and an answer that moves money (the review: $250 share / deposit refund) is never edited or cleared *(v: 2026-10-15)*.
- **#404** — re-invoking a confirmation-email handler to RESEND re-arms every reminder ladder it stamps. A reschedule must pass an explicit `reschedule` flag and every ladder-arming side effect must sit behind `!reschedule`; the failure is silent (a chase restarts on a form the recipient already has).
- **#650** — one failure, one `FAILURE_*` bell, with the client's NAME in the title (`clientLabel`) *(v: 2026-10-22)*. **#645** — a failure that reaches only the function log (kept 24 h) is never seen: every step that can lose a customer-facing email or document ends in a bell (the delivery layer retries Gmail then bells Jake), and a new money pipeline joins `automation_DOCS_missing_sweep` the day it ships *(v: 2026-10-21)*. **#365 / #179** — an action-required bell (`dismissible:false`) is a CONTRACT: a clear site for every satisfying path, a satisfied-on-fire guard, and a title nobody edits. Only the completing handler can clear it. **#176** — every bell goes through `notifyByRule` + a `notification_rules` seed. **#313** — one event serving two audiences needs TWO rule keys. **#481** is the mirror: one audience getting TWO bells about one event — when an action-required bell is raised for the same recipient about the same thing, make the raising function RETURN whether it fired, order it BEFORE the FYI, and drop that recipient (never the whole bell — the other recipients still need it). **#507 is the same rule read from the silence: a best-effort CHAIN that refuses is invisible — `console.error` is the only trace and nobody reads the function log, so Petrus's revised invoice sat refused for eight days.** Every chain whose failure a human must repair needs a bell beside the log line, with the ROUTINE skips (nothing to do, already done) staying silent so the bell keeps meaning something. **#509 is its client-facing twin: an EARLY RETURN in a client-click handler must still ANSWER the client.** The `$0` arm recorded the decision, belled the team and returned — truthfully reporting `email_drafted:false` — and the person who clicked the button got nothing; when you skip an email because its COPY is wrong for the case, the case needs its own copy, not silence.
- **#438** — a JS `String.replace` **replacement STRING** treats `$1`, `$&`, `` $` `` as capture references, so any money-bearing substitution silently corrupts itself (`$12,500.00` → the first capture group + `2,500.00`). **Every token substitution carrying a user- or DB-supplied value must use a replacer FUNCTION** (`() => value`), not a string. Bit twice in one branch.
- **#324 / #413** — a recipient ROLE TOKEN does NOTHING unless the HANDLER passes it (in template ctx, or in `notifyByRule`'s `dynamic` map), and it is dropped SILENTLY. **To know who receives a templated email or a bell, read the HANDLER — the template row and `notification_rules.default_recipients` are documentation, not routing.** A rule's `recipients` override wins only when non-empty AND resolvable, else the code default; the fallback direction is safe, keep it. **#503 is the same rule when a token must address SEVERAL people:** make the ctx value an ARRAY (`RecipientCtx` is `string | string[]`), widen **every producer of that token in the same change**, and census the producers by grepping the ONE helper they all call (`taxPlannerEmail(` → 36 ctx sites in 26 files), never by reading template rows — and check the OTHER channel, because `notifyByRule`'s `dynamic` map already accepted arrays, so bells and emails drifted apart silently for three weeks (the tax planner's allocated Team Member was on every bell and no email). **#505 is why you could not have caught it by testing:** the sandbox reroute hides recipients BY CONSTRUCTION, so resolve the REAL To/Cc/Bcc first and `console.log("sandbox: would have sent …")` before rerouting — non-sandbox output byte-identical — and read `function_logs` for that line. **#512 — the footer is a DELIVERY-LAYER invariant** (`utils/email-signature.ts` inside `draftGmail` / `gmailDraftFetch` / `deliverRaw`, idempotent on the sign-off text): no sender appends `VFO_SIGNATURE` any more, and a template body must not carry its own.
- **#411** — a bell's TITLE is the clear contract, so its RECIPIENT is FREE: re-address a live action-required bell by moving `defaultRecipients` and **leave the title (and the rule KEY) alone even when it now names the wrong role**. The clear site matches title-prefix + client_id and never reads recipient, so it retires both vintages with no cutover window. **#521 — a bell asks for a STEP, so it is addressed to that step's OWNER on every route:** when a step changes owner on one route (decision 29 gave the planner the client decision + pricing on Direct), grep every fire site by the step NAME and the title prefix and move them all — `TAX_client_decided_yes` kept following "the PF → the member" and asked the member for a form they could no longer see.
- **#356** — a template's Draft/Send mode is keyed by `(pipeline, template_name)` in TWO places per consumer; a mismatch looks exactly like a deliberately-Draft row and the mail sits in Drafts forever with no error. **#325** — **SEVENTY-ONE** rows are `send_mode=true` *(v: 2026-10-27, derive it — past values in CHANGELOG)*; editing one reaches a real person with nobody in between. **#428** — a `send_mode` flip is a **UI edit too**: grep the FE for every draft-era success string on that template's callers ("send the draft from Gmail" invites a duplicate real send), and give any real-sending admin button an idempotency stamp + a `force` override, with the guard BEFORE every side effect.
- **#382 / #392 / #323 / #459 / #554** — a user-visible LABEL can be a lookup key, a DISPLAYED name can diverge from the STORED name, and a task NAME can be exact-matched across both repos with no compile-time protection. Grep both repos before renaming anything. **#554: a BEHAVIOUR keyed on a record's display name is switched off by anyone who renames it in the UI** (Innovation's rename killed the vault-assess carve-out for five days) — put a flag on the record, never a string-literal name list. *(v: 2026-09-28)* **#540:** to rename a name the code MATCHES, add a display map (`src/components/shared/taxDisplayNames.js`) at the render sites only — never `UPDATE` the stored name; a bell TITLE is renamed the #411 way (new prefix + a `LEGACY_` prefix both clears retire — done 2026-09-25 for *Download the TPOM presentation* / *Confirm the TPOM happened*, superseding #540's "titles keep ROI") *(v: 2026-09-25)*. **#459, the render-side twin:** a mode-keyed CATCH-ALL row (`if (readOnly) return …`) placed mid-renderer silently preempts every branch below it and turns their own mode arms into dead code that reads as live — and it stayed invisible for a year because only ONE preempted step stores a **person's name** in its status column, where `statusColors[name]` misses and falls back to the "not done" grey. **Never feed a free-text column to a status→colour/label map**, and put a mode catch-all AFTER every branch that renders that mode itself.
- **#342** — an external asset the code reads BY NAME is coupled to the deploy: content-only assets hot-swap, structure-bearing ones need a NEW versioned name + a lockstep deploy, uploaded FIRST. **#555** — a switch that defaults OFF reads its OFF-side assets from the first request: every file and row the off branch names must be live BEFORE the deploy *(v: 2026-09-28)*. **#618** — a TAX agreement wording `UPDATE` (rows 25-32) also stales the eight `tax-agreements` sample PDFs (fetched by name): re-make them in the same change, and keep every row at 4 pages or the `field_map` signatures move *(v: 2026-10-14)*. **Diff against the object the LIVE FUNCTION READS, never against your build artifact** — 2026-08-18's template v4 was byte-identical to a backup already in the bucket and still needed a new version, because relative to the hot-swapped live object it restored a token SITE (#336). **#480** — **a wording change on a FIXED-WIDTH text box is a geometry change** (seven extra characters wrapped and overlapped the row below on three slides): widen by measurement AND set `wrap="none"`, replacing `<a:spAutoFit/>` with `<a:noAutofit/>` so an editor cannot re-fit the `cy` the handler's thresholds are measured against — and **when a part's shape SET changes, re-derive every per-part count the code asserts** (12/14 → 9/10) and keep a NEGATIVE CONTROL that fails the new constants against the old master. **#453** — an artifact that MIRRORS a money process must **import** that process's constants, never re-type them (a second copy of a number that must agree eventually disagrees, and the copy nobody executes is the stale one); and when two generation-time surgeries land on one part, the ORDER is a contract and each needs an assertion derivable **without** reference to the other, or a silently no-opping step is quietly agreed with by the check that should have caught it. **#538** — anything the ROI deck needs on every slide is a `<p:pic>` (the surgeries select `<p:sp>`), and member/group logos are shared media parts whose BYTES the generator swaps; shape counts cannot see a moved GROUP of shapes, so v9 also asserts geometry. **#542** — Google Slides re-wraps a text box PowerPoint does not: verify a generated slide in Google Slides, and take wrapping from the renderer (`wrap="none"`, box centred on the slide). **#547 (corrects #541)** — icon-font ligature TEXT renders as the literal words in Google Slides too: an icon in the master must be a picture (v11) *(v: 2026-09-25)*.
- **#407** — two asks answered at DIFFERENT times need DIFFERENT bell titles (one prefix = one clear contract); two keys may share a title only when the instruction is identical. To add a second ordering to a one-way flag, put a DISCRIMINATOR beside it (NULL = original) so everything keyed on the flag stays route-blind. **#588** — once two bells share a LINK, every dedupe / clear / latch keyed on that link must also name the title (the paid-lead FYI swallowed the action bell) *(v: 2026-10-07)*.
- **#452** — **SPLITTING an overloaded column into two silently invalidates every OTHER guard that read it as "somebody/anything is set", and the guards that break are the ones you did not touch.** Before splitting, grep every read and sort them into "meant the specific thing" (safe on the narrowed column) and "meant anybody" (must now test BOTH) — an error MESSAGE naming both meanings (*"Allocate a Team Member / Tax Planner"*) is the loudest tell that a guard is in the second group. A migration that MOVES rows out of the old column turns each such guard into an immediate live regression on exactly the rows it moved. Shipped that way in v793 and **found by the wrap-up DOC AUDIT, not by any gate** — the build, `deno check` and the live click-through all passed, because they exercised the new paths and never re-walked the old ones; fixed in v794.

**Treat docs as source of truth for architecture, then verify against code before changing anything** — docs drift on details, and when prose and a handler disagree, the handler wins (#399).

---

## VERIFICATION GATE + ENVIRONMENT

Commands live in **DERIVE AT SESSION START** above — this is only *when* each applies.

| Gate | Run it after | Pass |
|---|---|---|
| `deno check --no-lock` | any non-trivial backend change | **0 errors**; any error = fail |
| Action-count parity | any change near `dispatch.ts` / `index.ts` | matches your own pre-change count |
| `npm run build` | any `src/` change | exit 0 |
| Security advisor | **ANY** DB / table / policy / function change | the documented baseline exactly |
| 5-pipeline smoke gate | any `vfo-admin-api` deploy, or editing shared routing/dispatch/webhook/auth code | 5/5 PASS. Run as **superadmin** (Jake) — the five loaders are `SUPERADMIN_ONLY_ACTIONS`, so any other token reads as a false failure. Wiring check only: it asserts HTTP 200 + no `error`, and proves **nothing** about chain semantics, money math or bells. Skip for frontend-only, doc-only or isolated single-handler changes. |
| Live click-through | anything behavioural | the only gate that sees a bell, a stamp or an email fork — none of the above do |

**Environment** (Windows + PowerShell; full prose incl. every PowerShell trap → [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md), 2026-08-14 restructure heading)
- Deno (not on PATH): `C:\Users\jakel_fjetgbx\.deno\bin\deno.exe` · Supabase CLI: `C:\Users\jakel_fjetgbx\scoop\shims\supabase.exe`
- Frontend dev against the REAL backend: `cd C:\vfo-react\.claude\worktrees\<branch>; npm run dev` — **no `VITE_API_URL` override**, never `supabase start`, and `dev@local.test` is a local-only fake that must not be offered.
- Deploys (each needs explicit approval): `supabase functions deploy vfo-admin-api --project-ref ejpsprsmhpufwogbmxjv --use-docker` (Docker Desktop running, `deno.lock` deleted — #574) · `supabase functions deploy boldsign-webhook --no-verify-jwt` · `npm run deploy` (**this IS production** — vite build + gh-pages).
- **Bash is not on PATH; PowerShell has no `tail`; `sort -V` is NOT version-aware** (use `--sort=v:refname`, #222); `Set-Content`/`Out-File -Encoding utf8` write a **BOM** that leaks into commit subjects — prefer the Edit tool, or `[System.IO.File]::WriteAllText($f,$c,(New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding $false))`. `gh` is NOT installed.
